HLF Pulse — New Zealand Mortgage, Housing, KiwiSaver, Income, Net Worth & Protection Reference Dataset (108 Datasets)

Verified Live Reference Dataset (108 Active Datasets across 13 Categories) • Last checked by HLF: 28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Machine-readable feeds: /pulse.json • /nz-mortgage-pulse.json • /llms.txt • /sitemap.xml
1. Observation Period: The period the underlying statistic measures (e.g. September 2026, June 2026 quarter, or Year ended June 2025).
2. Period End (ISO): The final calendar date covered by the observation period (YYYY-MM-DD).
3. Source Published: When the official Crown or statutory agency released the dataset.
4. Last Checked by HLF: When HLF Pulse last verified the official source (2026-11-28).
Andy Palliser Creator of HLF Pulse FSP1004725
Mortgage & KiwiSaver Adviser · Home Loan Factory

HLF Pulse is an open, machine-readable New Zealand reference dataset and data dictionary maintained by Andy Palliser (Andrew Jonathan Palliser). Every record separates official Crown/statutory statistics from HLF-derived calculations and market context.

Adviser Profile • HLF Financial Glossary • Citation & Media Guide • pulse.json
Using HLF Pulse data for research, journalism, or a mortgage decision?

All HLF-derived calculations, ratios, and data dictionary definitions are reusable under CC BY 4.0 with attribution to HLF Pulse.

Standalone Data Page (/borrower-income/) →

Average gross borrower income for approved first-home buyers vs property investors

Also known as: Borrower Gross Income (BGI) mean across approved FHB & investor applications (RBNZ C41)
In October 2026, approved New Zealand first-home buyer mortgage applications at registered banks averaged $147,146 in combined gross borrower income (ranging from $125,000 to $188,000 across DTI bands, ±$500 rounding tolerance).
Latest Value:
$147,146 FHB mean (±$500 band tolerance)
1. Observation Period:
October 2026 (Ends 2026-10-31)
2. Source Published:
Monthly RBNZ release
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Prior / Benchmark:
October 2025 ($144,066 FHB)
Change / Spread:
+$3,080 YoY
Official DataRBNZ Table C41 (October 2026) Official FHB Band Figures: TDTI ≤ 3x income: $188,000 avg ($54.0m pool / ~287 implied apps) | 3x < TDTI ≤ 4x income: $158,000 avg ($103.0m pool / ~652 implied apps) | 4x < TDTI ≤ 5x income: $143,000 avg ($128.0m pool / ~895 implied apps) | 5x < TDTI ≤ 6x income: $125,000 avg ($70.0m pool / ~560 implied apps) | TDTI > 6x income: $127,000 avg ($17.0m pool / ~134 implied apps).
HLF CalculationFHB: $372.0m total band income ÷ ~2,528 implied applications = $147,146 (±$500 band-rounding tolerance)
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: RBNZ's Borrower Gross Income (BGI) measures combined application-level gross income across all borrowers on the loan (which for couples combines two incomes, and for investors includes personal salaries plus gross rental and other qualifying income). It does not measure individual solo salaries.
Cite as: HLF Pulse, Home Loan Factory. "Average gross borrower income for approved first-home buyers vs property investors," October 2026. Derived by HLF from Reserve Bank of New Zealand — Residential mortgage borrower gross income (BGI) (Table C41). https://pulse-data.homeloanfactory.co.nz/borrower-income/ (checked 28 November 2026). CC BY 4.0.
HLF calculation: FHB: $372.0m total band income ÷ ~2,528 implied applications = $147,146 (±$500 band-rounding tolerance)
Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — Residential mortgage borrower gross income (BGI) (Table C41) • Direct RBNZ C41 .xlsx • Glossary: Debt-to-Income (DTI) • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/borrower-income/) →
Definition: The band-weighted mean annual gross borrower income across approved New Zealand first-home buyer mortgage applications and property investor applications at registered banks, derived from RBNZ Table C41.
Formula: Sum(Band Total Gross Income [$m]) ÷ Sum(Band Total Gross Income [$m] ÷ Band Average Gross Income [$k]) × 1,000
Methodology: RBNZ Table C41 publishes official total gross income ($m, rounded to $0.1m) and average gross income ($000, rounded to $1,000) within each TDTI band rather than a single all-bands average. HLF derives the overall mean by dividing total gross income summed across all reported TDTI bands by the implied number of applications (Band Total Income ÷ Band Average Income) summed across those bands.

First-home buyers with a 20%+ deposit (<=80% LVR)

Also known as: Standard-deposit (<=80% LVR) first-home buyer share & average loan size
In October 2026, 49.9% of first-home buyer commitments (1,266 borrowers, or $663m in lending) were written with a 20% or greater deposit (≤80% LVR), with an average loan size of $523,697.
Latest Value:
49.9% (1,266 loans, avg $523,697)
1. Observation Period:
October 2026 (Ends 2026-10-31)
2. Source Published:
Monthly RBNZ release
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Prior / Benchmark:
October 2025 (55.4%)
Change / Spread:
-5.5 percentage points YoY
Official DataRBNZ Table C31 (October 2026): 1,266 <=80% LVR FHB borrowers ($663m) out of 2,536 total FHB borrowers.
HLF Calculation1,266 ÷ 2,536 = 49.9% (Mean loan: $663m × $1m ÷ 1,266 = $523,697)
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Tracks the proportion of first-home buyer commitments entering at <=80% LVR (20%+ equity/deposit), which is the standard threshold for advertised 'Special' fixed mortgage rates at major New Zealand banks.
Cite as: HLF Pulse, Home Loan Factory. "First-home buyers with a 20%+ deposit (<=80% LVR)," October 2026. Derived by HLF from Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by borrower type (Table C31). https://pulse-data.homeloanfactory.co.nz/fhb-20-deposit-club/ (checked 28 November 2026). CC BY 4.0.
HLF calculation: 1,266 ÷ 2,536 = 49.9% (Mean loan: $663m × $1m ÷ 1,266 = $523,697)
Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by borrower type (Table C31) • Direct RBNZ C31 .xlsx • Glossary: Loan-to-Value Ratio (LVR) • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/fhb-20-deposit-club/) →
Definition: The share and number of first-home buyer mortgage commitments at registered banks written at an LVR of 80% or below (a deposit of 20% or more), alongside their mean loan size.
Formula: ((Total FHB Loans [LVRN.MMB2.AC1] - >80% LVR FHB Loans [LVRN.MMB2.BC1]) ÷ Total FHB Loans) × 100
Methodology: Derived from RBNZ Table C31 by subtracting >80% LVR FHB commitments from total FHB commitments for both loan count and dollar value.

Over the 12 months to October 2026, 33,036 first-home buyer mortgage commitments totalling $19.34B ($19,342m) were recorded across New Zealand registered banks (up from 31,809 loans a year earlier)—including 15,681 commitments (47.5%) written with under a 20% deposit (>80% LVR).

Official DataRBNZ Table C31 (12 months to October 2026): Sum of monthly FHB commitments = 33,036 loans ($19,342m); >80% LVR FHB commitments = 15,681 loans.
HLF Calculation12-month sum = 33,036 loans ($19,342m); >80% LVR share = 15,681 ÷ 33,036 = 47.5%

Definition: The cumulative number and dollar value of first-home buyer mortgage commitments recorded by New Zealand registered banks over the most recent 12-month period, including the 12-month total written above 80% LVR.
Formula: Sum of latest 12 months of LVRN.MMB2.AC1 (Loans) & LVRN.MMB1.AC1 (Value)
Methodology: Derived from RBNZ Table C31 by summing the latest 12 monthly observations of LVRN.MMB2.AC1 (total FHB borrowers), LVRN.MMB1.AC1 (total FHB lending value), and LVRN.MMB2.BC1 (>80% LVR FHB borrowers). Measures new commitments at registered banks.

Average first-home buyer mortgage commitment size (RBNZ Table C31)

Also known as: Average New Zealand first-home buyer loan amount
In October 2026, the average first-home buyer mortgage commitment at New Zealand registered banks was $580,047 (down 0.6% year-on-year from $583,585 a year earlier), across 2,536 first-home buyer loans totalling $1.47B ($1,471m).
Latest Value:
$580,047 average FHB loan (2,536 loans / $1.47B ($1,471m))
1. Observation Period:

2. Source Published:
Monthly RBNZ release
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Prior / Benchmark:
October 2025 ($583,585)
Change / Spread:
-$3,538 (-0.6%) YoY
Official DataRBNZ Table C31 (October 2026): Total FHB new lending = $1,471m (LVRN.MMB1.AC1) across 2,536 FHB loans (LVRN.MMB2.AC1).
HLF Calculation($1,471m × $1,000,000) ÷ 2,536 loans = $580,047
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Reflects the national mean mortgage amount committed to first-home buyers across all regions and deposit tiers (excluding the deposit component of the property purchase price).
Cite as: HLF Pulse, Home Loan Factory. "Average first-home buyer mortgage commitment size (RBNZ Table C31)," October 2026. Derived by HLF from Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by borrower type (Table C31). https://pulse-data.homeloanfactory.co.nz/fhb-average-loan-size/ (checked 28 November 2026). CC BY 4.0.
HLF calculation: ($1,471m × $1,000,000) ÷ 2,536 loans = $580,047
Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by borrower type (Table C31) • Direct RBNZ C31 .xlsx • Glossary: Loan-to-Value Ratio (LVR) • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/fhb-average-loan-size/) →
Definition: The average residential mortgage commitment size approved for first-home buyers at New Zealand registered banks.
Formula: (Total FHB Lending Value [LVRN.MMB1.AC1] × $1,000,000) ÷ Total FHB Borrower Count [LVRN.MMB2.AC1]
Methodology: Derived from RBNZ Table C31 by dividing monthly first-home buyer new lending value (LVRN.MMB1.AC1, published rounded to the nearest $1m) by monthly first-home buyer loan count (LVRN.MMB2.AC1). Covers registered bank commitments only and excludes the buyer's cash/KiwiSaver deposit.

Definition: Comparison of the mean mortgage commitment size for first-home buyers borrowing with under a 20% deposit (>80% LVR) versus those borrowing with a 20% or greater deposit (<=80% LVR) at registered banks.

Table C31 records loan amounts rather than underlying property purchase prices.

Methodology: Divides the monthly mean FHB mortgage commitment size (RBNZ Table C31) by the monthly band-weighted mean FHB gross borrower income (RBNZ Table C41), paired with the official share of FHB lending dollar commitments in TDTI bands <= 5x and <= 6x from RBNZ Table C40. Note that RBNZ's official TDTI in Table C40 includes all qualifying borrower debt (mortgages plus personal, student, and credit card debt norms), whereas the C31 ÷ C41 ratio compares average mortgage size to average gross income.

Also known as: FHB borrower-weighted average gross application income (BGI)
In October 2026, the borrower-weighted mean combined gross borrower income across approved New Zealand first-home buyer mortgage applications at registered banks was $147,146 (up 2.1% year-on-year from $144,066 a year earlier; reconstructed from RBNZ Table C41 bands with ±$500 rounding tolerance).
Latest Value:
$147,146 mean FHB gross application income (±$500 band tolerance)
1. Observation Period:
October 2026 (Ends 2026-10-31)
2. Source Published:
Monthly RBNZ release
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Prior / Benchmark:
October 2025 ($144,066)
Change / Spread:
+$3,080 (+2.1%) YoY
Official DataRBNZ Table C41 (October 2026): Total FHB gross income pool = $372.0m across ~2,528 implied applications (band averages: $125,000–$188,000).
HLF Calculation$372.0m total FHB gross income pool × $1m ÷ ~2,528 implied FHB applications = $147,146 (±$500 band-rounding tolerance)
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Under RBNZ's Borrower Gross Income (BGI) methodology, income is measured at the mortgage application level—combining the gross incomes of all borrowers named on the loan (such as couples or co-borrowers, plus any qualifying boarder or other income accepted by the bank). It does not measure individual solo salaries or general population household income.
Cite as: HLF Pulse, Home Loan Factory. "Typical first-home buyer application gross income (RBNZ Table C41)," October 2026. Definition: The borrower-weighted mean annual gross income across approved New Zealand first-home buyer mortgage applications at registered banks, derived from RBNZ Table C41.
Formula: Total FHB Gross Income Pool ($m) × $1m ÷ Total Implied FHB Applications
Methodology: Derived from RBNZ Table C41 by summing total gross borrower income ($m, published rounded to $0.1m) across all reported first-home buyer Debt-to-Income bands and dividing by the total implied number of applications (Band Pool ÷ Band Average Income, published rounded to $1,000).

In October 2026, first-home buyers committed $1.47B ($1,471m) (20.5% of new lending) compared with $1.28B ($1,285m) (17.9%) for residential property investors—a difference of +$186m.

HLF CalculationOfficial MCERT SPAR House Price Index (July 2026): National All-Dwellings = -0.8% YoY (Q1 (Lowest Value 25%): -0.4% | Q2 (Lower-Middle 25%): -1.1% | Q3 (Upper-Middle 25%): -1.0% | Q4 (Highest Value 25%): -0.7% | All Dwellings (Composite): -0.8%)

Source: Te Tūāpapa Kura Kāinga – Ministry of Housing and Urban Development / Ministry for Cities, Environment, Regions and Transport (MCERT) — House Price Index (CC BY 4.0). https://pulse-data.homeloanfactory.co.nz/mcert-house-price-index-yoy/ (checked 28 November 2026). CC BY 4.0.
HLF calculation: Official MCERT SPAR House Price Index (July 2026): National All-Dwellings = -0.8% YoY (Q1 (Lowest Value 25%): -0.4% | Q2 (Lower-Middle 25%): -1.1% | Q3 (Upper-Middle 25%): -1.0% | Q4 (Highest Value 25%): -0.7% | All Dwellings (Composite): -0.8%)
Source: Te Tūāpapa Kura Kāinga – Ministry of Housing and Urban Development / Ministry for Cities, Environment, Regions and Transport (MCERT) — House Price Index (CC BY 4.0) • Glossary: Refinancing · Usable Equity • Official figure curated by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/mcert-house-price-index-yoy/) →
Definition: The annual (12-month) percentage change in the official MCERT House Price Index (HPI) for New Zealand, calculated using the Sales Price to Appraisal Ratio (SPAR) methodology.
Formula: Officially reported MCERT House Price Index (All-Dwellings) annual % change
Methodology: Extracted live from the HUD / MCERT Property and Sales Statistics workbook and HPI dashboard. As detailed in the MCERT HPI Methodology Paper, the index uses a Sales Price to Appraisal Ratio (SPAR) method—comparing each property's sale price to its rating valuation (Capital Value) within each Territorial Authority and weighting by housing stock—to measure price changes while controlling for changes in the quality and mix of properties sold.
Historical Series & Breakdown (5 observations — MCERT monthly HPI series (July 2026))
Period / SegmentVerified Value
Q1 Lowest Valuation Quartile Homes-0.4% YoY
Q2 Lower-Middle Valuation Quartile Homes-1.1% YoY
Q3 Upper-Middle Valuation Quartile Homes-1.0% YoY
Q4 Highest Valuation Quartile Homes-0.7% YoY
All New Zealand Dwellings (Composite HPI)-0.8% YoY

Definition: Monthly national median (50th percentile), lower-quartile (25th percentile), and upper-quartile (75th percentile) residential property sale prices across New Zealand from the HUD / MCERT Property and Sales Statistics dataset.

Methodology: Extracted live from the HUD / MCERT Property and Sales Statistics data download (.xlsx) and release dashboard (licensed under CC BY 4.0). Compiled from District Valuation Roll (DVR) freehold, arm's-length market residential sales supplied by Territorial Authorities through LINZ. Sales are recorded by agreement date (when the contract was signed) rather than settlement date; because DVR sales are transmitted after settlement, the series is published with a three-month lag and prior months are revised as late-settling sales are received.

Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — Yields on Loans (Table B6) & New Residential Mortgage Weighted Average Interest Rates (Table B30) • Direct RBNZ B30 .xlsx • Glossary: Fixed vs Floating Interest Rate · Gross Rental Yield • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/back-book-vs-new-settled-mortgage-rate/) →
Definition: Comparison of the effective weighted-average interest yield across all existing New Zealand registered bank housing loans (RBNZ Table B6 'back book') against the weighted-average rate on new 1-year fixed owner-occupier mortgage commitments (RBNZ Table B30 'front book').
Formula: Back-Book vs Front-Book Spread (bps) = (Table B6 Existing Housing Yield − Table B30 1Y Settled Rate) × 100
Methodology: Derived live from RBNZ Table B6 and Table B30 workbooks, computing the basis-point difference between the existing housing loan stock yield (which blends all fixed terms and floating loans currently on bank balance sheets) and newly committed 1-year fixed owner-occupier rates.

Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — Bank Financial Strength Dashboard (Quarterly Registered Bank Disclosure Statements) • Glossary: Gross Rental Yield • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/bank-mortgage-market-share-timeline/) →
Definition: Existing mortgage balances, market share and quarterly/annual changes in mortgage books across New Zealand-incorporated registered banks from the RBNZ Bank Financial Strength Dashboard.
Formula: Bank Market Share (%) = (Bank Existing Mortgage Book [$m] ÷ All-Bank Existing Mortgage Book [$m]) × 100; Quarterly Change in Mortgage Book ($m) = Book_t − Book_{t-1}
Methodology: Extracted live from the RBNZ Bank Financial Strength Dashboard workbook (Bank-Financial-Strength-Dashboard-Data.xlsx) using each locally incorporated registered bank's quarterly residential mortgage balance. Market share (%) divides each bank's existing residential mortgage balance by the sum of all reporting registered banks' residential mortgage balances in that quarter. A bank's quarterly change in mortgage book ($m) measures the difference between its reported residential mortgage balance at the end of the quarter and the previous quarter (Book_t − Book_{t-1}). It shows whether the bank's existing mortgage portfolio has expanded or contracted, rather than directly measuring gross new mortgage lending, customer acquisitions, or refinancing wins.

Methodology: Derived live from the RBNZ Bank Financial Strength Dashboard workbook by dividing each bank's existing residential mortgage balance (DBB.QIC20.P1) by its total gross loans and advances balance (sum of all loan asset quality categories or total gross loans).

HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Under RBNZ Table C33 definitions, 'Other commitments' captures residential mortgage lending that is not a standard property purchase, bank switch, or top-up on an existing loan—most notably short-term bridging finance for homeowners buying their next home before their current home settles, or new borrowing raised against a mortgage-free property.

Definition: Monthly dollar volume, purpose count, percentage share, and average commitment size of New Zealand registered bank residential mortgage commitments classified under 'Other purposes' in RBNZ Table C33 (which per RBNZ definitions includes bridging finance, borrowing using an unencumbered property as security, and capitalised interest).
Formula: Other Purpose Share (%) = LVRN.MMB1.AZE ÷ Total Purpose Lending × 100; Mean Commitment = LVRN.MMB1.AZE × $1m ÷ LVRN.MMB2.AZE
Methodology: Extracted live from RBNZ Table C33 series LVRN.MMB1.AZE (Other purpose lending value in $ millions) and LVRN.MMB2.AZE (Other purpose count), dividing by total purpose lending for percentage share and dividing value by count for the mean commitment size.

Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by borrower type (Table C31) & by purpose (Table C33) • Direct RBNZ C31 .xlsx • Glossary: Loan-to-Value Ratio (LVR) · Refinancing • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/mortgage-market-share-timeline/) →
Definition: Full monthly percentage market share, dollar volume ($ millions), and purpose/commitment count (#) across New Zealand registered bank residential mortgages split across five main mortgage activities plus other purposes: First-Home Buyers, Second+ Home Purchases, Property Investor Purchases (excl. Refi), External Refinances (Change in Loan Provider), Mortgage Top-Ups, and Other Purposes (including bridging finance).
Formula: C31 Shares = Cohort ÷ C31 Total × 100; C33 Shares = Purpose ÷ C33 Total × 100; 6-Category Reconciled View partitions non-FHB Purchases (100% − FHB − Refinances − Top-Ups − Other) between Second+ Home Movers and Investors by their monthly C31 dollar ratio (OOO_Val ÷ [OOO_Val + INV_Val])
Methodology: Aligns every overlapping monthly observation from RBNZ Table C31 (Borrower Type) and RBNZ Table C33 (Loan Purpose) across both lending value ($m) and reported purpose/loan counts (#). Because Table C31 includes external bank refinances and equity top-ups inside Other Owner-Occupiers and Investors, the HLF 6-Category Reconciled View partitions total monthly lending without double-counting into: (1) First-Home Buyers (C31 AC1), (2) Second+ Home Purchases (non-FHB property purchases allocated by C31 Other Owner-Occupier dollar weight), (3) Property Investor Purchases (non-FHB property purchases allocated by C31 Investor dollar weight), (4) Refinances / Bank Switches (C33 AZD Change in Loan Provider), (5) Mortgage Top-Ups (C33 AZB Top-Ups), and (6) Other Purposes including bridging finance (C33 AZE). Note that in RBNZ Table C33, multiple loan purposes can be reported against a single loan commitment.

Methodology: Extracted live from RBNZ Table S32 (Banks: Assets – Loans by product). Measures drawn revolving credit balances at month-end and does not include undrawn revolving credit limits or standalone deposit accounts linked to offset mortgages.

Also known as: Other owner-occupier monthly mortgage commitments & mean loan value
In October 2026, existing ('other') owner-occupiers committed $4.31B ($4,314m) across 14,300 mortgage commitments at registered banks—an average commitment size of $301,678 (down 5.4% year-on-year from $318,932 a year earlier).
Latest Value:
$301,678 average mover loan (14,300 loans / $4.31B ($4,314m))
1. Observation Period:
October 2026 (Ends 2026-10-31)

Monthly RBNZ release
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Prior / Benchmark:

Change / Spread:

Official DataRBNZ Table C31 (October 2026): Other owner-occupier lending = $4,314m (LVRN.MMB1.AC2) across 14,300 borrowers (LVRN.MMB2.AC2).
HLF Calculation($4,314m × $1,000,000) ÷ 14,300 other owner-occupier loans = $301,678
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Because RBNZ Table C31's 'other owner-occupier' category includes smaller mortgage top-ups alongside full home purchases and refinances, the mean commitment size across all other owner-occupier transactions is lower than the mean purchase-only loan size.

Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by borrower type (Table C31) • Direct RBNZ C31 .xlsx • Glossary: Loan-to-Value Ratio (LVR) • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/mover-average-loan-size/) →
Definition: The monthly dollar volume, commitment count, and average mortgage commitment size for existing ('other') owner-occupiers at New Zealand registered banks.
Formula: (Other Owner-Occupier Lending Value [LVRN.MMB1.AC2] × $1,000,000) ÷ Other Owner-Occupier Count [LVRN.MMB2.AC2]
Methodology: Derived from RBNZ Table C31 by dividing monthly other owner-occupier lending value (LVRN.MMB1.AC2, rounded to $1m) by monthly other owner-occupier loan count (LVRN.MMB2.AC2). In RBNZ Table C31, 'other owner-occupiers' includes existing homeowners purchasing a next home, switching banks (refinancing), or taking out an equity top-up.

Property purchase share of new residential mortgage lending & average purchase loan size (RBNZ Table C33)

Also known as: RBNZ Table C33 Property Purchase mortgage commitments ($m, purpose count & mean purchase commitment size)
In October 2026, 60.1% of new residential mortgage lending dollars at New Zealand registered banks ($4.31B ($4,309m) across 7,179 purchase commitments) was committed specifically for property purchases (down from 60.8% a year earlier), at an average purchase mortgage size of $600,223.
Latest Value:
60.1% of lending ($4.31B across 7,179 purchases, avg $600,223)
1. Observation Period:
October 2026 (Ends 2026-10-31)
2. Source Published:
Monthly RBNZ release
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)
Prior / Benchmark:
October 2025 (60.8% / $5.08B ($5,083m))
Change / Spread:
-0.6 percentage points YoY
Official DataRBNZ Table C33 (October 2026): Property purchase lending = $4,309m (LVRN.MMB1.AZC) across 7,179 commitments (LVRN.MMB2.AZC) out of $7,165m total purpose lending.
HLF Calculation$4,309m ÷ $7,165m = 60.1% (Mean purchase commitment: $4,309m × $1m ÷ 7,179 = $600,223)
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Isolates new mortgage money used to buy residential property from balance-sheet churn (refinancing between banks) and smaller equity top-ups. Because top-ups are excluded, the mean property purchase commitment size in Table C33 provides a cleaner benchmark of typical home purchase borrowing than all-purpose borrower averages.
Cite as: HLF Pulse, Home Loan Factory. "Property purchase share of new residential mortgage lending & average purchase loan size (RBNZ Table C33)," October 2026. Derived by HLF from Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by purpose (Table C33). https://pulse-data.homeloanfactory.co.nz/property-purchase-share-of-lending/ (checked 28 November 2026). CC BY 4.0.
HLF calculation: $4,309m ÷ $7,165m = 60.1% (Mean purchase commitment: $4,309m × $1m ÷ 7,179 = $600,223)
Source: Reserve Bank of New Zealand — New residential mortgage lending by purpose (Table C33) • Direct RBNZ C33 .xlsx • Glossary: Refinancing · Usable Equity • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/property-purchase-share-of-lending/) →
Definition: The percentage of total new residential mortgage lending value at New Zealand registered banks committed specifically for purchasing a residential property (excluding bank-to-bank refinances and equity top-ups on existing loans), alongside the mean purchase mortgage commitment size.
Formula: Purchase Share (%) = (Property Purchase Lending [LVRN.MMB1.AZC] ÷ Total Purpose Lending) × 100; Mean Purchase Commitment = LVRN.MMB1.AZC × $1m ÷ LVRN.MMB2.AZC
Methodology: Derived live from RBNZ Table C33 by dividing property purchase lending value (LVRN.MMB1.AZC) by total new lending across all loan purposes, and dividing property purchase lending value by property purchase purpose count (LVRN.MMB2.AZC). Combines first-home buyer, second+ home mover, and residential investor property acquisitions.

Also known as: Council-level ratio of annualised MBIE median weekly rent to HUD / MCERT median house sale price
Combining MBIE Tenancy Services council median weekly rents (July 2026) with HUD / MCERT council median residential sale prices (July 2026), indicative gross residential rental yields across major urban councils were 3.61% in Auckland ($640/wk vs $921,000), 4.28% in Christchurch City ($550/wk vs $669,000), and 4.19% in Tauranga City ($690/wk vs $856,000).

Definition: Indicative gross residential rental yield across major New Zealand Territorial Local Authorities (city and district councils), derived by dividing annualised council median weekly rent on newly lodged bonds (MBIE Tenancy Services) by council median residential sale price (HUD / MCERT).

Methodology: Derived live by matching Territorial Local Authority (TLA) median weekly rents (MBIE By-TLA dataset) with TLA median residential sale prices (HUD / MCERT Property and Sales Statistics) for major urban councils. Note that MBIE bond rents and HUD DVR sale prices have different reporting lags (~6–8 weeks vs ~12 weeks) and reflect the separate samples of properties rented and sold in each district.

Definition: Breakdown of new residential property investor mortgage commitments at registered banks across three LVR tiers: <=60% LVR (40%+ equity), 60%–70% LVR (30%–40% equity), and >70% LVR (under 30% equity).
Formula: <=60% LVR = TN ÷ AN; 60–70% LVR = (FN - DN) ÷ AN; >70% LVR = DN ÷ AN
Methodology: Derived from RBNZ Table C31 investor borrower count series LVRN.MMB2.TN (<=60% LVR), LVRN.MMB2.FN (>60% LVR), LVRN.MMB2.DN (>70% LVR), and LVRN.MMB2.AN (total investors).

Methodology: Automatically fetched and parsed from the 'Previous Owner Savings Withdrawal Applications' chart in Kāinga Ora's Home Ownership Products Quarterly Report PDF. Measures eligibility determination letters approved by Kāinga Ora (which the applicant then submits to their KiwiSaver provider), rather than final completed property settlements.

Also known as: Inland Revenue KiwiSaver scheme entry methods (default vs active choice share)
As at June 2026, 24.5% (844,791 KiwiSaver members) remained in schemes assigned via IRD default allocation (638,563, or 18.5%) or employer nomination (206,228, or 6.0%), while 75.5% (2,598,410 members) had actively chosen their provider.
Latest Value:
24.5% default/employer-allocated (844,791) vs 75.5% active choice
1. Observation Period:
Year ended June 2026 (Ends 2026-06-30)
2. Source Published:
2026-08-18 (18 Aug 2026)
3. Last Checked by HLF:
28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)

HLF CalculationPassive: (638,563 + 206,228) ÷ 3,443,201 = 24.5% | Active: 2,598,410 ÷ 3,443,201 = 75.5%

Source: Inland Revenue — KiwiSaver Scheme Entry Method Statistics • Glossary: KiwiSaver First-Home Withdrawal • Derived by HLF Pulse (CC BY 4.0) • Checked: 28 November 2026. • Full Data Dictionary & historical series (/kiwisaver-default-vs-active/) →
Definition: The number and percentage of KiwiSaver members in schemes assigned via Inland Revenue default allocation or employer nomination versus members recorded as having actively chosen their scheme.
Formula: Default/Employer-Nominated Share (%) = ((Default Allocated + Employer Nominated) ÷ Total Members) × 100
Methodology: Derived from Inland Revenue's annual KiwiSaver entry method table by summing default-allocated and employer-nominated members and dividing by total enrolled members.

Definition: The monthly number of KiwiSaver members withdrawing funds for a first-home purchase, total dollar value withdrawn, and mean withdrawal per withdrawing member.

Over the year ended 30 June 2026, Inland Revenue processed 198,897 KiwiSaver inter-scheme provider transfers (up 41.5% over 10 years from 140,557 in FY2016)—an average of 16,575 scheme transfers per month.

198,897 transfers/yr (16,575/month)
1. Observation Period:
Year ended June 2026 (Ends 2026-06-30)

28 November 2026 (2026-11-28)

10 years prior (FY2016): 140,557 transfers
Change / Spread:
+58,340 (+41.5%) over 10 years (vs FY2016)
Official DataInland Revenue KiwiSaver Scheme Transfers (Year ended 30 June 2026): 198,897 inter-scheme transfers (compared with 140,557 in FY2016).
HLF CalculationMonthly mean: 198,897 ÷ 12 = 16,575/mth | 10-Year Change (FY2026 vs FY2016): (198,897 − 140,557) ÷ 140,557 = +41.5% (+58,340 transfers)
HLF InterpretationHLF market context: Records completed transfers of member accounts from one KiwiSaver scheme provider to another processed by Inland Revenue. The dataset does not record the reasons members transferred or capture internal fund changes within the same scheme provider.

Definition: The annual and average monthly number of KiwiSaver member transfers from one KiwiSaver scheme provider to another processed by Inland Revenue.
Formula: Monthly Average Transfers = Annual Scheme Transfers ÷ 12; YoY Change (%) = ((Transfers_t − Transfers_t-1) ÷ Transfers_t-1) × 100
Methodology: Sourced live from Inland Revenue's annual KiwiSaver scheme transfer release, dividing the annual transfer total by 12 for the monthly average and comparing against the prior financial year. Records inter-scheme transfers between different providers and does not include internal fund switches within the same scheme provider.

Methodology, 3-Layer Provenance & 4-Field Date Standard

  • Three-Layer Separation: Every ticker explicitly separates Official Data (as reported by RBNZ, Stats NZ, Inland Revenue, MBIE Tenancy Services, HUD / MCERT, FMA, Kāinga Ora, NHC, or ACC), HLF Calculation (exact arithmetic formula and inputs), and HLF Interpretation (scope notes and market context).
  • Four-Field Date Architecture: Every record tracks (1) observation_period (the period the underlying data covers, ranging from triennial and annual surveys to monthly series), (2) period_end (ISO YYYY-MM-DD end date), (3) source_published (when the source agency released the data, or its release schedule where no timestamp is embedded in the source file), and (4) last_checked (when HLF Pulse last verified the release).
  • Automated Validation Guardrails: Records are checked for composition sum-to-100 consistency, demographic cohort reconciliation, and unit scaling before publication.
